\big\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{4}\right)I_{k}.

Thus

\displaystyle(i)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\left[\kappa_{y,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{4}\right)-1\right]I_{k}+(iii).

(72)

The tube-closure bound gives

\big\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{32},

so

\kappa_{y,k}^{2}=\left(1-\big\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\right)^{-2}.

Using
T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}\leq 1/4
, the elementary scalar inequality

\frac{1}{(1-s)^{2}}\left(1+\frac{a}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{3a}{4}\right)\leq 1-\frac{a}{8},\qquad 0\leq s\leq\frac{a}{32},\quad 0\leq a\leq\frac{1}{4},

with
a=T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}
, yields

\kappa_{y,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{4}\right)\leq 1-\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{8}.

(73)

This replaces the long anchor-switch display and keeps the expression within the page margins.
Combining (
72
) and
(
73
), we obtain

(i)\leq-\frac{\lambda_{k}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{8}I_{k}+O\!\left(\xi^{2}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}T}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}+O\!\left(\frac{(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\right)\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

(74)

We next control the exact block
(ii)
. By
Corollary
4
,

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\kappa_{z,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}+\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{8}+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{32}\right)\big\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}-\lambda_{k}J_{k}+(iv),

(75)

where

(iv):=O\!\left(\xi^{2}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\lambda_{k+1}\alpha_{k}^{2}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(76)

Using
\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}\leq T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}/16
and
\beta_{k}\leq\gamma_{k}
, the parenthesis
in (
75
) is bounded by
1+T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}/2
. Hence

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\kappa_{z,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{2}\right)\big\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}-\lambda_{k}J_{k}+(iv).

(77)

By Corollary
3
,

\big\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{4}\right)J_{k}.

Therefore

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\left[\kappa_{z,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{4}\right)-1\right]J_{k}+(iv).

(78)

The tube-closure bound gives

\big\|y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{32}.

Using the same scalar inequality as above with
a=T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}
, we get

\kappa_{z,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{4}\right)\leq 1-\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{8}.

(79)

This is the compact replacement for the long expression that was previously overflowing the page.
Thus

(ii)\leq-\frac{\lambda_{k}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{8}J_{k}+O\!\left(\xi^{2}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2},

(80)

where we used
\lambda_{k+1}\alpha_{k}^{2}=O(\alpha_{k}\beta_{k})
, which follows from

\lambda_{k+1}=\lambda_{k}+\delta_{k}
,
\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}=O(\beta_{k})
, and

\beta_{k}=\alpha_{k}\lambda_{k}
.

Substituting (
74
) and (
80
) into
(
68
) gives

\displaystyle V_{k+1}-V_{k}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{2}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+O\!\left(\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\left[1-O\!\left(\frac{\xi\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}T}\right)-O\!\left(\frac{\xi\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\beta_{k}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}T\gamma_{k}}\right)\right]\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{8}I_{k}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{16}J_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+O\!\left(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\right)\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

(81)

The last term in the
barrier-aware
schedule is chosen so that

\frac{\xi\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}T}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\xi\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\beta_{k}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}T\gamma_{k}}

are sufficiently small. Hence the bracket multiplying
\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}
is nonnegative, and the
entire
q_{k}^{x}
-term can be discarded. We arrive at

\displaystyle V_{k+1}-V_{k}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{2}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{8}I_{k}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{16}J_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+O\!\left(\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+O\!\left(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\right)\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

(82)

This is exactly (
66
).
∎

Remark 5
.

We state Assumption
1
globally in the upper variable to avoid trajectory-dependent
regularity assumptions. The same proof of Proposition
5
can be localized to any compact outer region on which the
assumed derivative bounds hold. A sufficient condition for such a region is boundedness of the
relevant sublevel sets of
F_{\mu}
: the Lyapunov descent keeps
F_{\mu}(x_{k})
below the initial
Lyapunov value plus the finite schedule remainder, so the generated outer iterates remain in a
bounded sublevel region.

G.3
Proof of Theorem
3

We now derive the deterministic rate from the Lyapunov descent inequality. Theorem
2

ensures that the exact and proxy target neighborhoods are forward invariant under the
barrier-aware
schedule,
so Proposition
5
applies at every outer iteration. After dropping the
negative tracker-error terms, the Lyapunov recursion contains only two summable residuals:
the proxy-gradient bias term
\alpha_{k}/\lambda_{k}^{2}
and the multiplier-growth term

\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}^{2}
. The polynomial schedule in Theorem
3
is chosen
so that these residuals are summable up to logarithmic factors while

\sum_{k<K}\alpha_{k}
grows as
K^{2/3}
, yielding the claimed

\widetilde{O}(K^{-2/3})
stationarity rate.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

By Theorem
2
, the exact and proxy target neighborhoods are forward invariant.
Hence Proposition
5
applies for every
k
. Dropping the negative
tracker terms in that lemma gives

V_{k+1}-V_{k}\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{2}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+O\!\left(\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+O\!\left(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\right)\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

Summing from
k=0
to
K-1
, and using
V_{K}\geq\inf_{x}F_{\mu}(x)
, gives

\frac{\xi}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}\leq V_{0}-\inf_{x}F_{\mu}(x)+O\!\left(\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+O\!\left(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

For the polynomial schedule,

\alpha_{k}=\frac{\alpha_{0}}{(k+k_{0})^{1/3}},\qquad\lambda_{k}=\lambda_{0}\left(\frac{k+k_{0}}{k_{0}}\right)^{1/3},

we have

\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=\frac{\alpha_{0}k_{0}^{2/3}}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}\frac{1}{k+k_{0}},

and therefore

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=O(\log K).

Moreover, since
\delta_{k}=\lambda_{k+1}-\lambda_{k}
and the
barrier-aware
schedule gives

\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}\leq 1/64
,

\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{k}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{k+1}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}\right)\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{64}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{k}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{k+1}}\right).

Thus

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{64}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{K}}\right)=O(1).

Also,

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\geq\alpha_{K-1}K=\frac{\alpha_{0}K}{(K+k_{0})^{1/3}}=\Omega(K^{2/3}).

Therefore,

\min_{0\leq k\leq K-1}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}}=\widetilde{O}(K^{-2/3}).

Equivalently, to obtain

\min_{0\leq k\leq K-1}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\epsilon
, it suffices to take

K=\widetilde{O}(\epsilon^{-3/2})
. This proves Theorem
3
.
∎

Appendix H
Stochastic Extension

For the upper-level-noise-only extension, we assume that only the
f
-gradients are stochastic.

Assumption 2

(Upper-level stochastic first-order oracles)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
denote the sigma-field generated by the algorithmic history before an oracle query.
For every
(x,y)
measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}
, we access stochastic
f
-gradients

\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x,y;\zeta),\qquad\widehat{\nabla}_{x}f(x,y;\zeta),

such that

\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x,y;\zeta)\mid\mathcal{F}]=\nabla_{y}f(x,y),\qquad\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\nabla}_{x}f(x,y;\zeta)\mid\mathcal{F}]=\nabla_{x}f(x,y),

and

\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x,y;\zeta)-\nabla_{y}f(x,y)\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\sigma_{f,y}^{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}[\|\widehat{\nabla}_{x}f(x,y;\zeta)-\nabla_{x}f(x,y)\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\sigma_{f,x}^{2}.

The lower objective
g
, the barrier
\phi
, and hence
\psi_{\mu}=g+\mu\phi
, are deterministic.

Assumption 3

(Local almost-sure bounds on sampled upper-level gradients)
.

There exist finite constants
\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}
and

\bar{\ell}_{f,y,0}^{\eta}
such that, on the maintained-tube event, for every visited

x\in X
and every

y\in T_{2\eta,\mu}(x)\cup T_{2\eta,\mu}^{\lambda}(x)
,

\|\widehat{\nabla}_{x}f(x,y;\zeta)\|_{2}\leq\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}\quad\text{a.s.},

and

\|\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x,y;\zeta)\|_{*}\leq\bar{\ell}_{f,y,0}^{\eta}\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\|\cdot\|_{*}
denotes the anchored Dikin dual norm associated with the relevant exact or
proxy center.

Stochastic tube-closure condition.

The stochastic schedule is chosen so that the almost-sure oracle perturbations in
Assumption
3
are dominated by the exact and proxy tracker contraction
budgets. Under this condition, the exact and proxy target neighborhoods remain forward invariant along the
stochastic trajectory.

H.1
Tube Invariance under Upper-level Noise

We first show that the tube invariance argument is stable under upper-level stochastic noise, provided
the sampled perturbations are smaller than the contraction margins of the two trackers. The result is
pathwise: on every realization satisfying the local almost-sure bounds in
Assumption
3
and the stochastic closure inequalities below, the exact and
proxy target neighborhoods remain forward invariant.

For the stochastic algorithm, the exact tracker is unchanged, while the proxy tracker and outer
direction use

\widehat{\nabla}_{y}L_{\lambda_{k},\mu}(x_{k},y):=\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x_{k},y;\zeta)+\lambda_{k}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},y),

and

\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}:=\widehat{\nabla}_{x}f(x_{k},y_{k+1};\zeta_{k}^{x})+\lambda_{k}\bigl(\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},y_{k+1})-\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},z_{k+1})\bigr).

Thus
x_{k+1}=x_{k}-\xi\alpha_{k}\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}
. Let

\Gamma_{\psi,k}:=1-(1-\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k})^{T},\qquad\Gamma_{L,k}:=1-\left(1-\frac{7\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\beta_{k}\right)^{T},\qquad\beta_{k}:=\alpha_{k}\lambda_{k}.

Define the pathwise upper-level noise budgets

\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}:=\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}+\bar{\ell}_{f,y,0}^{\eta},\qquad\bar{Q}_{k}:=\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0}.

Here
\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}
bounds the sampled
y
-gradient perturbation in the relevant anchored
Dikin dual norm, and
\bar{Q}_{k}
bounds the sampled outer direction.

Proposition 6

(Stochastic tube invariance)
.

Suppose Assumption
1
, Assumption
2
, and
Assumption
3
hold. Suppose also that the deterministic
barrier-aware conditions hold, and that for every
k
,

\displaystyle T\alpha_{k}\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\Gamma_{L,k},

(83)

\displaystyle\ell_{*,0}^{\eta}\xi\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{0}}+2\ell_{g,0}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\Gamma_{\psi,k},

(84)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{8\lambda_{0}}+\xi\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\left(\frac{\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{0}}+2\ell_{g,0}\right)\right)\beta_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\Gamma_{L,k}.

(85)

If the initial warm starts satisfy

z_{0}\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{0})
and

y_{0}\in T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{0}}(x_{0})
, then, on the pathwise event where the sampled-gradient
bounds in Assumption
3
hold, for every outer iteration
k
, all exact
inner iterates remain in
T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k})
, all proxy inner iterates remain in

T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})
, and the next warm starts satisfy

z_{k+1}\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k+1}),\qquad y_{k+1}\in T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{k+1}}(x_{k+1}).

Thus the exact and proxy target neighborhoods are forward invariant along the stochastic trajectory.

Proof.

The proof is by induction over the outer iteration
k
. Assume that

z_{k}\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k})
and

y_{k}\in T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})
. We prove that all inner iterates remain in the corresponding
target neighborhoods and that the next warm starts lie in the target neighborhoods at
x_{k+1}
.

Exact tracker.

The exact tracker does not use stochastic gradients, since
g
,
\phi
, and
\psi_{\mu}
are
deterministic. Therefore the deterministic fixed-center contraction applies:

\|z_{k}^{(t+1)}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}\leq(1-\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k})\|z_{k}^{(t)}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}.

Since
z_{k}^{(0)}=z_{k}\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k})
, all exact inner iterates remain in

T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k})
. Moreover,

\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\sqrt{1-\Gamma_{\psi,k}}\,\|z_{k}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\left(1-\frac{\Gamma_{\psi,k}}{2}\right)\eta.

Proxy tracker under sampled
y
-gradient noise.

Let
y_{k}^{(t)}
be a proxy inner iterate, and write

\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}:=\widehat{\nabla}_{y}f(x_{k},y_{k}^{(t)};\zeta_{k,t}^{y})-\nabla_{y}f(x_{k},y_{k}^{(t)}).

On the proxy neighborhood, Assumption
3
and the deterministic
local
f
-bound give

\|\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\leq\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}.

The proxy update equals the deterministic proxy update plus the perturbation

-\alpha_{k}\nabla^{2}\phi(y_{k})^{-1}\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}
. By the frozen-metric/center-anchor comparison in
Appendix
E
, after absorbing fixed
\eta
-dependent constants,

\|\nabla^{2}\phi(y_{k})^{-1}\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\|\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\leq\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}.

Combining this with the deterministic one-step contraction gives

\|y_{k}^{(t+1)}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\sqrt{1-\frac{7\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\beta_{k}}\,\|y_{k}^{(t)}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}+\alpha_{k}\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}.

(86)

The condition (
83
) implies, in particular,

\alpha_{k}\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\cdot\frac{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{2}\beta_{k}.

Thus, if
y_{k}^{(t)}\in T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})
, then

\|y_{k}^{(t+1)}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\left(1-\frac{7\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{16}\beta_{k}\right)\eta+\frac{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{16}\beta_{k}\eta\leq\eta.

Hence all proxy inner iterates remain in
T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})
. Iterating
(
86
) over
T
inner steps gives

\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\sqrt{1-\Gamma_{L,k}}\,\|y_{k}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}+T\alpha_{k}\bar{\sigma}_{y}^{\eta}.

Using (
83
) and

\sqrt{1-\Gamma_{L,k}}\leq 1-\Gamma_{L,k}/2
, we obtain

\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{8}\Gamma_{L,k}\right)\eta.

Sampled outer movement.

By Assumption
3
and the deterministic bound on

\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}=\nabla_{x}g
,

\|\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}\|_{2}\leq\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0}.

Therefore

\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}\leq\xi\alpha_{k}\bigl(\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0}\bigr)\leq\xi\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{0}}+2\ell_{g,0}\right).

(87)

Exact center shift.

By local stability of the exact minimizer map and (
87
),

\|y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\ell_{*,0}^{\eta}\xi\beta_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{0}}+2\ell_{g,0}\right)\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\Gamma_{\psi,k},

where the last inequality is (
84
). Combining this drift bound with
the old-center contraction estimate for
z_{k+1}
, and applying the self-concordant anchor-switch
inequality exactly as in the deterministic tube invariance proof, yields

\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})}\leq\eta.

Proxy center shift.

By the proxy minimizer stability bound,

\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\frac{2\delta_{k}\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k+1}\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}+\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}.

Using the deterministic multiplier-growth condition

\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}\leq T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}/16
,
\lambda_{k+1}\geq\lambda_{k}\geq\lambda_{0}
, and
(
87
), we get

\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\left(\frac{T\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{8\lambda_{0}}+\xi\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\left(\frac{\bar{\ell}_{f,x,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{0}}+2\ell_{g,0}\right)\right)\beta_{k}.

By (
85
), this is at most

\eta\Gamma_{L,k}/8
. Combining this proxy-center drift bound with the old-center estimate

\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\left(1-\frac{3}{8}\Gamma_{L,k}\right)\eta

and applying the same anchor-switch argument as above gives

\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})}\leq\eta.

Thus the next warm starts lie in the target neighborhoods. This closes the induction and proves the forward
invariance of the exact and proxy target neighborhoods along the stochastic trajectory.
∎

H.2
Expected Lyapunov Descent

We now derive the stochastic analogue of Proposition
5
. The proof follows
the deterministic Lyapunov argument, with two additional noise contributions. First, stochastic

\nabla_{y}f
-queries in the proxy tracker add a variance term to the proxy fixed-center contraction.
Second, the stochastic outer direction perturbs the motion of
x_{k}
, but its linear contribution has
zero conditional mean; only the quadratic smoothness and center-drift remainders contribute in
expectation.

For each outer iteration
k
, let
\mathcal{F}_{k}
denote the sigma-field
generated by the algorithmic history at the beginning of iteration
k
, and
for each inner step
t\in\{0,1,\ldots,T\}
, let
\mathcal{F}_{k,t}
denote
the sigma-field generated by the history through the start of the
t
-th
proxy inner step, so that
\mathcal{F}_{k,0}=\mathcal{F}_{k}
. After the inner
loops are completed, let
\mathcal{G}_{k}
denote the sigma-field generated by
the history immediately before sampling the outer stochastic gradient, so that

\mathcal{F}_{k,T}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{k}
. Write

\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}=q_{k}^{x}+\varepsilon_{k}^{x},\qquad\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{k}^{x}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=0,\qquad\mathbb{E}[\|\varepsilon_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq\sigma_{f,x}^{2},

where

q_{k}^{x}:=\nabla_{x}f(x_{k},y_{k+1})+\lambda_{k}\bigl(\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},y_{k+1})-\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},z_{k+1})\bigr)

is the conditional mean proxy direction. The stochastic outer update is

x_{k+1}=x_{k}-\xi\alpha_{k}\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}
. For the stochastic proxy tracker, write

\widehat{\nabla}_{y}L_{\lambda_{k},\mu}(x_{k},y)=\nabla_{y}L_{\lambda_{k},\mu}(x_{k},y)+\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y},\qquad\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k,t}]=0.

By the Euclidean–Dikin conversion on the maintained proxy neighborhood, the
variance assumption implies

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}\,\Big|\,\mathcal{F}_{k,t}\right]\leq\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}

for a finite
\eta
-dependent constant
\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}
. Set

\sigma_{\eta}^{2}:=\sigma_{f,x}^{2}+\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}.

Proposition 7

(Expected Lyapunov descent)
.

Suppose Assumption
1
, the stochastic oracle assumptions, and the stochastic
tube-maintenance conditions hold. Let

I_{k}:=\big\|y_{k}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2},\qquad J_{k}:=\big\|z_{k}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2},

and

V_{k}:=F_{\mu}(x_{k})+\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}I_{k}+\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{2}J_{k}.

Then there exist finite constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
, independent of
k
and
K
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]-V_{k}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{16}I_{k}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{32}J_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{1}\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{2}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

(88)

Consequently, after dropping the negative tracker terms,

\mathbb{E}[V_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]-V_{k}\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+C_{1}\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{2}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

(89)

Proof.

Stochastic proxy fixed-center contraction.

Fix
k
and condition on the history before the
t
-th proxy inner step. The stochastic update is
the deterministic proxy step plus the perturbation

-\alpha_{k}\nabla^{2}\phi(y_{k})^{-1}\varepsilon_{k,t}^{y}
. By the frozen-metric/center-anchor reduction from
Appendix
E
,
and the same deterministic contraction estimate used in Lemma
5
,
the noiseless part of the proxy step satisfies the one-step bound with factor

1-\frac{7\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\beta_{k}
. The martingale-difference property removes the linear
noise term, and the quadratic noise term is bounded by the conditional variance. Hence

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|y_{k}^{(t+1)}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|^{2}_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k,t}\right]\leq\left(1-\frac{7\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\beta_{k}\right)\|y_{k}^{(t)}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|^{2}_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}+C\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}.

Repeating this for
t=0,\ldots,T-1
, using

T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}\leq 1/4
, and summing the geometric noise terms gives

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|^{2}_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\leq\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{4}\beta_{k}\right)I_{k}+CT\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}.

(90)

Expected outer descent.

Condition on
\mathcal{G}_{k}
. By
L_{F}^{\eta}
-smoothness of
F_{\mu}
,

F_{\mu}(x_{k+1})\leq F_{\mu}(x_{k})-\xi\alpha_{k}\langle\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k}),\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}\rangle+\frac{L_{F}^{\eta}\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}}{2}\|\widehat{q}_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

Taking conditional expectation and using

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{k}^{x}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=0
gives

\mathbb{E}[F_{\mu}(x_{k+1})-F_{\mu}(x_{k})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq-\xi\alpha_{k}\langle\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k}),q_{k}^{x}\rangle+\frac{L_{F}^{\eta}\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}}{2}\bigl(\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{f,x}^{2}\bigr).

Using
\xi L_{F}^{\eta}\alpha_{k}\leq 1/2
, the same algebra as in
Proposition
4
, and the proxy-gradient bias bound from
Proposition
2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F_{\mu}(x_{k+1})-F_{\mu}(x_{k})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\Bigl(2\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{16}\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{64}\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}.

(91)

Expected moving-anchor recursions.

The moving-anchor recursions from Appendix
E
are pathwise deterministic estimates
once the outer displacement is fixed. In the stochastic setting,

x_{k+1}-x_{k}=-\xi\alpha_{k}(q_{k}^{x}+\varepsilon_{k}^{x}).

In the Taylor expansions for the exact and proxy center shifts, the terms linear in

\varepsilon_{k}^{x}
have conditional mean zero because the relevant test vectors are

\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable. The quadratic remainders contribute only

O(\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,x}^{2})
. Thus the scheduled moving-anchor recursions become

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{k+1}I_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\kappa_{y,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{4}\beta_{k}\right)\|y_{k+1}-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}+C\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}},

(92)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{k+1}J_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{k}\kappa_{z,k}^{2}\left(1+\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\gamma_{k}\right)\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}.

(93)

Here the constants absorb the local dikin stability constants. The factor
\lambda_{k}
in the
variance terms comes from the Lyapunov scaling and the fact that

\lambda_{k+1}=O(\lambda_{k})
under the multiplier-growth condition.

Combining with fixed-center contraction.

Substitute (
90
) into
(
92
). The same scalar anchor-switch absorption used in
Proposition
5
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{k+1}I_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\beta_{k}\right)\lambda_{k}I_{k}+C\frac{\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]

\displaystyle\quad+C\lambda_{k}T\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}+C\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}.

(94)

For the exact tracker, the fixed-center contraction is deterministic, since
\psi_{\mu}
is exact:

\|z_{k+1}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}^{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{3T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{4}\gamma_{k}\right)J_{k}.

Combining this with (
93
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{k+1}J_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{8}\gamma_{k}\right)\lambda_{k}J_{k}+C\frac{\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}}{T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]

\displaystyle\quad+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}.

(95)

Lyapunov difference.

Using the definition of
V_{k}
, combine
(
91
), (
94
), and
(
95
). The terms involving

\mathbb{E}[\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]
are absorbed by the negative

-(\xi\alpha_{k}/4)\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}
term exactly as in the deterministic proof, using the

barrier-aware
smallness condition on
\xi/T
and the relation
\beta_{k}\leq\gamma_{k}
. This gives
negative tracker terms, a proxy-bias remainder, a multiplier-growth remainder, and the accumulated
variance terms:

C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}+C\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}\sigma_{f,x}^{2}+C\lambda_{k}T\alpha_{k}^{2}\sigma_{f,y,\eta}^{2}.

Since
\lambda_{k}\geq\lambda_{0}>0
and
T,\xi,\lambda_{0}
are fixed constants, these are bounded by

C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]-V_{k}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\beta_{k}}{16}I_{k}-\frac{\lambda_{k}\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}T\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\gamma_{k}}{32}J_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{1}\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{2}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

This proves (
88
). Dropping the negative tracker terms gives
(
89
).
∎

H.3
Proof of Theorem
4

We now prove the stochastic convergence rate. The proof is the stochastic analogue of the
deterministic telescoping argument: tube maintenance ensures that the local Dikin estimates remain
valid, while the expected Lyapunov recursion contributes one additional summable variance term.

Proof.

By Proposition
6
, the exact and proxy target neighborhoods are forward
invariant along the stochastic trajectory under the stochastic tube-closure condition. Hence the local
Dikin estimates from Propositions
1
–
2

and the expected Lyapunov descent in Proposition
7
apply at every
outer iteration.

From (
89
), taking total expectation gives

\mathbb{E}[V_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}]\leq-\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}+C_{1}\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{2}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

(96)

Summing (
96
) from
k=0
to
K-1
, and using

V_{K}\geq\inf_{x}F_{\mu}(x)
, yields

\displaystyle\frac{\xi}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq V_{0}-\inf_{x}F_{\mu}(x)+C_{1}\xi(c_{x}^{\eta})^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}+C_{3}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}.

(97)

We now bound the three residual sums under the polynomial stochastic schedule

\alpha_{k}=\frac{\alpha_{0}}{(k+k_{0})^{3/5}},\qquad\gamma_{k}=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{(k+k_{0})^{2/5}},\qquad\lambda_{k}=\lambda_{0}\left(\frac{k+k_{0}}{k_{0}}\right)^{1/5},\qquad\delta_{k}=\lambda_{k+1}-\lambda_{k}.

First,

\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=\frac{\alpha_{0}k_{0}^{2/5}}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{k+k_{0}},

and therefore

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=O(\log K).

(98)

Second,

\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}=\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}{k_{0}^{1/5}}\cdot\frac{1}{k+k_{0}},

so

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}^{2}\lambda_{k}=O(\log K).

(99)

Third, since
\lambda_{k+1}=\lambda_{k}+\delta_{k}
,

\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}=\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{k}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{k+1}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}\right).

The multiplier-growth condition gives
\delta_{k}/\lambda_{k}\leq c
for a fixed numerical constant

c<1
. Hence

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}\leq(1+c)\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{k}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{k+1}}\right)\leq\frac{1+c}{\lambda_{0}}=O(1).

(100)

Finally,

\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}=\alpha_{0}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(k+k_{0})^{-3/5}=\Theta(K^{2/5}).

(101)

Combining (
97
)–(
101
), we obtain

\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}}\leq\widetilde{O}(K^{-2/5}).

Since the minimum is bounded by the weighted average,

\min_{0\leq k\leq K-1}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}}{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\alpha_{k}}=\widetilde{O}(K^{-2/5}).

This proves the claimed stochastic stationarity rate. Equivalently, choosing

K=\widetilde{O}(\epsilon^{-5/2})
gives an
\epsilon
-stationary point of the
barrier-smoothed objective
F_{\mu}
, in the sense

\min_{0\leq k<K}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x_{k})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\epsilon
.
∎

Appendix I
Experimental Details

In this section, we provide details for the numerical experiments in
Section
4
, together with some additional results. All experiments were run on a 16-inch MacBook Pro equipped with an Apple M4 Pro
chip and 48 GB of unified memory.

I.1
Motivating Example

Motivating example: boundary stability

We first describe the two-dimensional example used to visualize Dikin tube maintenance. The feasible
region is a fixed irregular hexagonal polytope
Y=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:Ay\leq b\}
. Its halfspace
representation is constructed from the polygon vertices, with outward normals normalized to unit
length. We equip
Y
with the logarithmic barrier

\phi(y)=-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\log s_{i}(y),s_{i}(y):=b_{i}-a_{i}^{\top}y.
We set
\mu=5\times 10^{-4}
and use the quadratic lower objective

g(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}(y-c(x))^{\top}Q(y-c(x)),\qquad Q=\begin{pmatrix}1.2&0.15\\
0.15&0.8\end{pmatrix}.

The center path is linear,

c(x)=(1-x)c_{\mathrm{start}}+xc_{\mathrm{end}},
where
c_{\mathrm{start}}
lies inside the polytope and
c_{\mathrm{end}}
lies beyond the right
slanted face. Thus, as
x
increases, the exact barrierized minimizer

y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)=\arg\min_{y\in\operatorname{int}(Y)}\psi_{\mu}(x,y),\qquad\psi_{\mu}(x,y):=g(x,y)+\mu\phi(y),

moves toward an active face while remaining strictly feasible. We discretize
x\in[0,1]
into
K=2000
outer grid points
x_{k}
. For each
x_{k}
, the exact
center
y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})
is computed numerically by minimizing over a softmax barycentric
parametrization of the polygon interior, warm-started from the previous solution. At each outer index

k
, both trackers are initialized from their previous state and run for
T=30
inner steps. The Euclidean baseline uses the plain exact-tracker update

z_{k}^{(t+1)}=z_{k}^{(t)}-\gamma_{\mathrm{E}}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},z_{k}^{(t)}),

with fixed step size

\gamma_{\mathrm{E}}=0.7\,\gamma_{\mathrm{crit}}(0),\quad\gamma_{\mathrm{crit}}(k)=\frac{2}{\lambda_{\max}(\nabla_{yy}^{2}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})))}.

The quantity
\gamma_{\mathrm{crit}}(k)
is used only as a reference local Euclidean stability scale.
The Euclidean step size is chosen from the initial, well-conditioned part of the path and then held
fixed as the exact center moves toward the boundary. The barrier-metric tracker uses the frozen Dikin preconditioner from Algorithm
1
.
At outer index
k
, update

z_{k}^{(t+1)}=z_{k}^{(t)}-\gamma_{k}\nabla^{2}\phi(z_{k})^{-1}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},z_{k}^{(t)}).

The scalar
\gamma_{k}
is chosen conservatively, following the
barrier-aware
schedule principle.
The exact centers
y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})
are not used to choose the implementable steps; they are
computed only for visualization and post-hoc verification.

After generating the trajectories, we evaluate the anchored Dikin error

\|z_{k}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}=\Bigl((z_{k}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}))^{\top}\nabla^{2}\phi(y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}))(z_{k}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}))\Bigr)^{1/2}

and check whether it remains below the neighborhood radius
\eta=0.25
, namely whether

z_{k}\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x_{k}):=\left\{z\in\operatorname{int}(Y):\|z-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\eta\right\}.

This neighborhood condition provides a post-hoc certificate of whether the generated trajectory remains in
the local Dikin region. The comparison shows that the fixed Euclidean tracker loses tube control near
the active face, whereas the barrier-metric tracker remains consistent with the moving local Dikin
geometry.

Figure
2
reports four quantities. Panel (a) plots the exact centers

y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})
, the Euclidean tracker, the barrier-metric tracker, and the first Euclidean tube
exit. Panel (b) plots the auxiliary outer value
f(x_{k},z_{k})
, together with the exact-center value

f(x_{k},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}))
. Panel (c) plots the anchored Dikin error and marks the tube radius

\eta
. Panel (d) plots the exact lower objective gap

\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},z_{k})-\psi_{\mu}(x_{k},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}))
.
The Euclidean tracker is shown until it leaves the maintained tube near the active face; the
barrier-metric tracker remains feasible and tube-controlled throughout the path.

Figure 5:

Interior stability experiment for the exact lower tracker.
(a) The Euclidean tracker oscillates and drifts under a step size above the local Euclidean stability
threshold, while the barrier-metric tracker remains stable.
(b) The auxiliary objective decreases for the barrier-metric tracker but eventually increases for the
Euclidean tracker.
(c) The anchored Dikin error contracts under the barrier-metric and grows under the Euclidean step.
(d) The exact lower objective gap decays for the barrier-metric tracker, while the Euclidean gap
increases after a transient phase.

Additional experiment: interior stability.

We also include an auxiliary experiment in which the exact barrierized minimizer is far from the
boundary. In contrast to the active-face example above, here the outer parameter is fixed at

x_{0}=0.5
. This removes center drift and isolates the stability of the lower tracker under different
local geometries. We use the same two-dimensional polyhedral setup as in the main motivating example. Let

Y=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:Ay\leq b\}
, with logarithmic barrier
\phi(y)=-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\log s_{i}(y),s_{i}(y):=b_{i}-a_{i}^{\top}y.

For the fixed outer parameter
x_{0}
, consider the exact barrierized lower objective

\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},y)=\frac{1}{2}(y-c)^{\top}Q(y-c)+\mu\phi(y),

where
Q\succ 0
is anisotropic and
\mu>0
is small. The center
c
and the polytope are chosen
so that

y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})=\arg\min_{y\in\operatorname{int}(Y)}\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},y)

lies well inside
Y
. We set the slack:

\min_{i}s_{i}(y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0}))\approx 6.51,

so the observed behavior is not caused by proximity to an active face. Starting from a common interior point
z_{0}
at Euclidean distance about
1
from

y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})
, we compare two exact lower trackers for the same objective

\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},\cdot)
. The Euclidean tracker uses

z_{t+1}=z_{t}-\gamma_{\mathrm{E}}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},z_{t}),

where
\gamma_{\mathrm{E}}
is chosen slightly above the local Euclidean stability threshold

\gamma_{\mathrm{crit}}=\frac{2}{\lambda_{\max}(\nabla_{yy}^{2}\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})))}.

The barrier-metric tracker uses

z_{t+1}=z_{t}-\gamma_{\mathrm{B}}\nabla^{2}\phi(z_{t})^{-1}\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},z_{t}).

Figure
5
reports the trajectory, the auxiliary objective value

f(x_{0},z_{t})
, the anchored Dikin error

\|z_{t}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})}=\Big((z_{t}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0}))^{\top}\nabla^{2}\phi(y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0}))(z_{t}-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0}))\Big)^{1/2},

and the exact lower objective gap
\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},z_{t})-\psi_{\mu}(x_{0},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{0})).
Although all iterates remain feasible and the minimizer is strictly interior, the Euclidean tracker
becomes unstable once its step size exceeds the local Euclidean stability threshold. In contrast, the
barrier-metric tracker steadily reduces both the Dikin error and the lower objective gap. This example
shows that the barrier-metric is useful not only for preventing boundary exits, but also for stabilizing
lower-level tracking under anisotropic local curvature.

I.2
Congestion Toll Design

Main experiment.

We provide the details of the synthetic congestion-toll benchmark used in Section
4
.
For each pair
(n,\mathrm{seed})
, we generate an independent toll-design instance using

numpy.random.default_rng(seed)
. The number of shared bottlenecks is

m_{b}=\max\{5,\lfloor n/10\rfloor\}.

The incidence matrix
C\in\{0,1\}^{m_{b}\times n}
is generated by assigning each corridor to
between one and three uniformly sampled bottlenecks, followed by a correction step ensuring that
every bottleneck is used by at least one corridor. Corridor capacities are sampled as
u_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0.8,1.2]
, and the total demand cap is set
to

D=0.6\sum_{i}u_{i}.

We initialize the lower variable at the strictly interior point
y_{\mathrm{int}}=0.15u
, rescaling if
necessary so that
\mathbf{1}^{\top}y_{\mathrm{int}}<0.5D
. The bottleneck capacities are then chosen
to make this point strictly feasible while preserving random tightness. Specifically, with

r:=Cu
and
c:=Cy_{\mathrm{int}}
, we first sample

\widetilde{d}_{r}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0.45,0.65]\,r_{r}
and set

d_{r}=\max\left\{\widetilde{d}_{r},\,\frac{1.35\,c_{r}+10^{-3}}{\tau}\right\}.

This guarantees
Cy_{\mathrm{int}}<\tau d
with a fixed margin for every generated instance. The baseline travel costs are sampled as
\ell_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0.5,2.0]
. The congestion matrix is

Q=\operatorname{diag}(q)+VV^{\top}/n,

where
q_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0.1,0.5]
and
V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 3}
has entries

V_{ij}\sim N(0,0.25^{2})
. We use

\kappa=1,\beta=1,\rho_{\mathrm{rev}}=10^{-2},\rho_{x}=10^{-3}.

All methods are initialized with

x_{0}=(0.5,\ldots,0.5)^{\top},

and the tolls are constrained by
0\leq x\leq 10
. The target revenue is set to

R_{\mathrm{tar}}=0.25D\,\operatorname{mean}(x_{0}).

In the main experiment, we use bottleneck tightness
\tau=0.20
, barrier parameter

\mu=10^{-3}
, and dimensions

n\in\{50,100,200,400,600,800,1000,1200\}.
Each method receives a
2
-second optimization wall-clock budget per
instance. Exact objective evaluations used only for reporting are not counted against this optimization
budget. We compare five methods. BMFO is our proposed barrier-metric first-order method.
Exact-HG-Barrier solves the barrier-smoothed lower problem accurately and applies the exact implicit
hypergradient. CVXPY-HG solves the original lower quadratic program using CVXPY and
differentiates the active-set KKT system (
Agrawal et al. 2019
). Khanduri-DSIGD is a smoothed implicit-gradient baseline
inspired by
Khanduri et al. 2023
. Kornowski-F2CBA is a first-order linearly constrained
bilevel baseline inspired by
Kornowski et al. 2024
, implemented with a warm-started first-order
lower oracle. For BMFO, the barrier-metric directions require solving linear systems with the
lower-level log-barrier Hessian. In the reported experiments, we use a dense
Cholesky solve for smaller instances and switch to a preconditioned
conjugate-gradient (PCG) solve for larger instances. For the normalized gap in Figure
3
(b), we distinguish the barrier-smoothed objective from the original
constrained bilevel objective. Given a toll vector
x
, we define

F_{\mathrm{orig}}(x):=f(x,y^{\star}(x)),\qquad y^{\star}(x)\in\arg\min_{y\in Y(\tau)}g(x,y),

where
Y(\tau)
is the original polyhedral feasible set without the log-barrier
term. We evaluate
F_{\mathrm{orig}}
by solving this lower-level convex QP to
high accuracy and then plugging the resulting response
y^{\star}(x)
into the
upper objective
f
. Thus
F_{\mathrm{orig}}
measures performance on the
original constrained traffic-response problem. For each instance
(n,\mathrm{seed},\tau)
, the reference value

F_{\mathrm{ref}}
is the best
F_{\mathrm{orig}}
value found by the offline
reference pool. Panel (b) reports

\frac{F_{\mathrm{orig}}(x_{\mathrm{final}})-F_{\mathrm{ref}}}{\max\{10^{-4},10^{-3}|F_{\mathrm{ref}}|\}}.

Values below one meet the target tolerance, and values above
1.2
are clipped
only for visualization.

Additional results.

Figures
6
–
7
report additional congestion-toll
experiments varying the bottleneck tightness and wall-clock budget. Figure
6

uses a slacker feasible region,
\tau=0.30
, under the same 2-second optimization budget as the main
experiment. Figure
7
compares
\tau=0.20
and
\tau=0.30
under a
larger 4-second budget. Across these variants, BMFO retains low per-update cost as the lower
dimension grows and remains competitive in normalized original-objective gap, while exact or
implicit-gradient baselines become increasingly expensive at larger dimensions.

Figure 6:

Congestion-toll benchmark with bottleneck tightness
\tau=0.30
and a
2
-second optimization
wall-clock budget per method.

Figure 7:

Congestion-toll benchmark with bottleneck tightness
\tau=0.20,0.30
and a
4
-second optimization
wall-clock budget per method.

I.3
Constrained MNIST Hypercleaning

Main experiment.

We use a constrained MNIST hypercleaning benchmark. For each random seed

s\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
, we load the standard MNIST training and test splits using

torchvision
, normalize pixel intensities to
[0,1]
, and vectorize each image as

\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{784}
. We apply a seed-dependent random permutation to the
60{,}000

training examples: the first
n=19{,}000
examples form the noisy training set, and the next

m=1{,}000
examples form the clean validation set. We also keep
5{,}000
randomly permuted
test examples for evaluation. Training labels are corrupted independently with probability
p
. If an example with label

y\in\{0,\ldots,9\}
is selected for corruption, its label is replaced by

(y+u)\bmod 10
, where
u\sim{\rm Unif}\{1,\ldots,9\}
, so the corrupted label is always
different from the original one. Validation and test labels are kept clean. For each seed, BMFO and
F2CBA use the same train/validation/test split and the same corrupted training labels. Let

\mathcal{D}_{\rm train}=\{(\tilde{\xi}_{i},\tilde{y}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n},\mathcal{D}_{\rm val}=\{(\xi_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{m}.
The upper variable
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
assigns the training weight

\sigma(\lambda_{i})\in(0,1)
to example
i
. The lower variable

w\in\mathbb{R}^{dK_{\rm cls}}
is the vectorized parameter of a

K_{\rm cls}=10
-class softmax-regression model, with
d=784
. The lower problem is

w^{\star}(\lambda)\in\arg\min_{w\in\mathcal{W}_{\tau}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma(\lambda_{i})\ell_{\rm CE}(\tilde{\xi}_{i},\tilde{y}_{i};w)+\frac{\rho}{2}\|w\|_{2}^{2}\right\},

and the upper objective is the validation cross-entropy

F(\lambda)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{\rm CE}(\xi_{j},y_{j};w^{\star}(\lambda)).

The lower feasible set combines coordinatewise box constraints with patchwise signed-mass
constraints. We divide the
28\times 28
image grid into
4\times 4
non-overlapping

7\times 7
patches
\{\mathcal{P}_{r}\}_{r}
, and impose

\mathcal{W}_{\tau}:=\left\{w:|w_{q,c}|\leq R\ \forall q,c,\qquad\left|\sum_{q\in\mathcal{P}_{r}}w_{q,c}\right|\leq\tau b_{r,c}\ \forall r,c\right\}.

Here
R>0
is the coordinatewise box radius,
b_{r,c}>0
is the patchwise mass scale, and

\tau\in(0,1]
controls constraint tightness. In our implementation, we use

R=2,b_{r,c}=|\mathcal{P}_{r}|=7\cdot 7=49,\tau=0.2,
so the patchwise signed-mass threshold is

\tau b_{r,c}=0.2\cdot 49=9.8

for every patch
r
and class
c
. Equivalently, the code uses

patch_size=7
and patch threshold
9.8
. The initialization
w=0
is strictly feasible
for both the box and patchwise constraints. Figure
4
uses
p=0.2
;
Figure
8
reports the higher-corruption case
p=0.4
.

Additional results.

Figure
8
reports the same constrained MNIST hypercleaning
experiment with a higher label corruption probability
p=0.4
. The qualitative behavior is consistent
with the
p=0.2
setting: F2CBA decreases the validation loss quickly at the beginning but plateaus,
whereas BMFO continues to improve and reaches a lower final validation loss. The same trend holds
when performance is measured by the number of gradient evaluations, indicating that the improvement
is not only an iteration-count effect.

Figure 8:

Constrained MNIST hypercleaning with label corruption probability
p=0.4
.
Panel (a) reports validation cross-entropy versus algorithm iterations, and panel (b) reports
validation cross-entropy versus the number of gradient evaluations. Under the higher corruption
level, BMFO continues to reduce validation loss after F2CBA plateaus, and attains a lower final loss
both per iteration and per gradient evaluation.

Appendix J
Other Supporting Lemmas

This appendix collects auxiliary estimates used in the proxy-bias and tracker-recursion arguments.
Lemma
11
gives a local linearization of the proxy direction around
the exact barrierized center, which is the main algebraic tool behind the
O(1/\lambda)

proxy-gradient bias. Lemma
12
records the local smoothness of
the proxy minimizer map
x\mapsto y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)
. The final two lemmas,
Lemmas
13
and
14
, turn exact and
proxy center drift into inner-product bounds against arbitrary tracker-error vectors; these are used to
control the cross terms in the moving-anchor recursions.

J.1
Proxy-gradient Linearization

Lemma 11

(Local proxy-gradient linearization)
.

Fix
\lambda>0
and
x\in X
. For any
y\in T_{\eta,\mu}(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla F_{\mu}(x)-\nabla_{x}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)+\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\nabla_{y}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\Big(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\Big)\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\Big(\ell_{f,1}^{\eta}+\lambda\min\{2\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta},\,\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\}\Big).

(102)

Proof.

Fix
x\in X
. Since

\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))=0
and

\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\succ 0
on the maintained tube, the implicit function
theorem gives

Dy_{\mu}^{\star}(x)=-\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)).

(103)

Hence, by the chain rule,

\displaystyle\nabla F_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))+\big(Dy_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\big)^{\top}\nabla_{y}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))-\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\nabla_{y}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)).

(104)

Next, using

L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)=f(x,y)+\lambda(\psi_{\mu}(x,y)-\psi_{\mu}^{\star}(x))
and

\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}^{\star}(x)=\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}f(x,y)+\lambda\big(\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)-\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big),

\displaystyle\nabla_{y}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{y}f(x,y)+\lambda\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y).

(105)

Combining (
104
)–(
105
) gives

\displaystyle\nabla F_{\mu}(x)-\nabla_{x}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)+\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\nabla_{y}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\nabla_{x}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))-\nabla_{x}f(x,y)}_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\big(\nabla_{y}f(x,y)-\nabla_{y}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\underbrace{\Big(\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)-\nabla_{x}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))-\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big)}_{R_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\underbrace{\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}}_{\text{linear correction}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\underbrace{\Big(\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)-\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))-\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big)}_{\widetilde{R}_{y}}.

(106)

The linear
\lambda\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))
terms cancel.

Operator-norm in the anchored Dikin geometry:

We will use the induced norms

\|M\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}:=\sup_{\|u\|_{2}=1}\|Mu\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*},\qquad\|M\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\to 2}:=\sup_{\|v\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}=1}\|Mv\|_{2}.

By duality,

\|M^{\top}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\to 2}=\|M\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}.

Proposition
1
implies

\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\leq\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta},

(107)

and therefore

\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\to 2}\leq\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}.

Also, since

\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\succeq\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}H_{\mu}^{\star}(x),

we have

\big\|\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}w\big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\|w\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}.

(108)

Combining the preceding two displays yields

\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\Big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*\to 2}\leq\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}.

(109)

Bound
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
.

By the local bound for
f
, with Euclidean–Dikin conversion factors absorbed into

\ell_{f,1}^{\eta}
,

\|T_{1}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{f,1}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}.

(110)

For
T_{2}
, combine (
109
) with the local Lipschitz bound for

\nabla_{y}f
:

\|T_{2}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\|\nabla_{y}f(x,y)-\nabla_{y}f(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\leq\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\ell_{f,1}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}.

Since
\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}/\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\geq 1
, (
110
) gives

\|T_{1}\|_{2}+\|T_{2}\|_{2}\leq 2\Big(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\Big)\ell_{f,1}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}.

(111)

Bound
R_{x}
and
\widetilde{R}_{y}
.

For
R_{x}
, the integral remainder form gives

\displaystyle R_{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)+t(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)))-\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big)^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\cdot(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\,dt.

(112)

By duality, for any matrix
M
,

\|M^{\top}(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\|_{2}\leq\|M\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}.

Thus

\displaystyle\|R_{x}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)+t(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)))

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt-\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\,dt.

(113)

On the tube, either use the crude bound

\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,\cdot)\|_{2\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\leq\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}
twice, or use the
Hessian-Lipschitz-in-
y
bound with constant
\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}
. Hence

\|R_{x}\|_{2}\leq\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\min\Big\{2\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta},\,\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\Big\}.

(114)

Similarly, since
\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))=0
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{y}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)-\nabla_{y}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))-\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)+t(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)))-\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\cdot(y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\,dt.

(115)

Using the corresponding operator norm

\|\cdot\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\to y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}
, we get

\|\widetilde{R}_{y}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}\leq\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\min\Big\{2\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta},\,\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\Big\}.

(116)

Finally, applying (
109
) to the linear correction multiplying

\widetilde{R}_{y}
, we obtain

\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\widetilde{R}_{y}\Big\|_{2}\leq\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\|\widetilde{R}_{y}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x),*}.

(117)

Since
\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}/\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}\geq 1
, combining
(
114
)–(
117
) gives

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\|R_{x}\|_{2}+\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}\psi_{\mu}(x,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x))\big)^{-1}\widetilde{R}_{y}\Big\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\Big(\frac{\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\Big)\lambda\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\min\Big\{2\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta},\,\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\|y-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\Big\}.

(118)

Taking Euclidean norms in (
106
), applying the triangle inequality,
and using (
111
) and (
118
), we obtain
(
102
).
∎

J.2
Local Smoothness

Lemma 12

(Local smoothness of
x\mapsto y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)
)
.

Fix
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
. For each
x\in X
, let

y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)
denote the unique minimizer of

y\mapsto L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)
, and assume the maintained tube condition

y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)\in T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda}(x)
for all

x\in X
.

Then the map
x\mapsto y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)
is differentiable on

X
, and its Jacobian satisfies

\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)=-\Big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x))\Big)^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)).

(119)

Moreover, there exists a finite local constant
\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}<\infty
such that,
for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
,

\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})}\leq\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2}.

(120)

One admissible choice is

\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}:=32\left(\frac{\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}}{\lambda}+\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\right)\frac{(\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}.

(121)

Uniformly over
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, one may replace

\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}/\lambda
by
\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}/\lambda_{0}
.

Proof.

Fix
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
. By optimality, for each visited
x
,

\nabla_{y}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x))=0.

By Proposition
1
and
Lemma
3
, the Hessian

\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x,y)
is positive definite on

T_{\eta,\mu}^{\lambda}(x)
. Hence the implicit function theorem implies differentiability of

x\mapsto y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)
and yields
(
119
).

Using (
119
) at
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=-\Big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))\Big)^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1}))\Big)^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})).

(122)

To compare the two terms, multiply the difference by

\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))
and add/subtract the usual
cross term. Using the lower curvature bound

\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y)\succeq\frac{\lambda\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{2}H_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{2}\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))-\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1}))\Big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}),*}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))-\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1}))\Big\|_{y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}),*}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}.

(123)

The local Lipschitz-Hessian bounds for
L_{\lambda,\mu}
give

\Big\|\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))-\nabla^{2}_{xy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1}))\Big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}),*}

\leq(\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}+\lambda\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta})\Big(\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2}+\|y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\|_{y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}\Big),

and the same bound holds for the Hessian difference

\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{2},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}))-\nabla^{2}_{yy}L_{\lambda,\mu}(x_{1},y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1}))

in the corresponding
y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2}),*
operator norm.
By Proposition
2
(i) with fixed
\lambda
,

\|y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\|_{y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}\leq\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2},

after absorbing the fixed anchor-switch constant. Also,

\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x)}\leq\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta},

again by the local stability of the proxy minimizer map. Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}{2}\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq(\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}+\lambda\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta})(1+\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta})^{2}\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2}.

(124)

Rearranging gives

\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}\leq\frac{2(\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}+\lambda\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta})}{\lambda\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}(1+\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta})^{2}\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2}.

Using the proxy stability bound

\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\leq\frac{3\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta}}{\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}},

and increasing the absolute constant if necessary, we obtain the bound:

\big\|\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})-\nabla y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})\big\|_{2\to y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})}\leq 32\left(\frac{\ell_{f,2}^{\eta}}{\lambda}+\ell_{\psi,2}^{\eta}\right)\frac{(\ell_{\psi,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{3}}\|x_{2}-x_{1}\|_{2}.

(125)

Finally, applying the self-concordant anchor-switch comparison between

y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{2})
and
y_{\lambda,\mu}^{\star}(x_{1})
only changes the constant by a fixed

\eta
-dependent factor, which is absorbed into

\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}
. This gives (
120
).
∎

Convention on anchor-switch constants.

The constants
\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}
in Definition
1
(
S3
) and
\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}
in Lemma
12
are only required to be finite
upper bounds in the exact and proxy center-shift estimates. Hence, without loss of generality, we
enlarge
\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}
so that
\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}\geq\max\{1,\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}\}.
With this convention, the single constant
\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}
appearing in the
barrier-aware
schedule
controls both the exact and proxy anchor-switch remainders.

J.3
Cross Bound

Lemma 13

(Cross bound for exact-center drift)
.

Fix an outer iteration
k
, and let
v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}
be any vector chosen before updating

x_{k}
. We use the following local bounds for the exact minimizer map

x\mapsto y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)
.

(J0)

(Lipschitz continuity).

There exists a finite constant
\ell_{*,0}^{\eta}<\infty
such that, for all

x\in X
and all
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
,

\|\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)u\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x)}\leq\ell_{*,0}^{\eta}\|u\|_{2}.

(126)

(J1)

(Smoothness).

There exists a finite constant
\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}<\infty
such that

\Big\|y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\frac{\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}}{2}\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

(127)

Then, for any
j_{k}>0
,

\displaystyle\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Bigl(\xi\alpha_{k}j_{k}+\frac{(\ell_{*,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{2}\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr)\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigl(\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4j_{k}}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{4}\alpha_{k}^{2}\Bigr)\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(128)

Proof.

We decompose the exact-center displacement into its linear part and Taylor remainder:

\displaystyle\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\big\langle v_{k},\,\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\big\rangle}_{(I)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\Big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\rangle}_{(II)}.

(129)

Since
x_{k+1}-x_{k}=-\xi\alpha_{k}q_{k}^{x}
, we have

(I)=-\xi\alpha_{k}\big\langle v_{k},\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})q_{k}^{x}\big\rangle.

By Cauchy–Schwarz in the anchored Dikin primal/dual pair and
(
126
),

|(I)|\leq\xi\alpha_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\|\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})q_{k}^{x}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\xi\alpha_{k}\ell_{*,0}^{\eta}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}.

Applying Young’s inequality with parameter
j_{k}>0
gives

(I)\leq\xi\alpha_{k}j_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}+\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4j_{k}}(\ell_{*,0}^{\eta})^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(130)

By (
127
) and Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\Big\|y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ell_{*,1}^{\eta}}{2}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

(131)

Using
x_{k+1}-x_{k}=-\xi\alpha_{k}q_{k}^{x}
and the bound

\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{f,0}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0},

we have

\displaystyle\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr).

(132)

Combining (
131
) and
(
132
), and applying Young’s inequality to the remaining product,
yields

(II)\leq\frac{(\ell_{*,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{2}\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr)\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{4}\alpha_{k}^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(133)

Combining (
130
) and (
133
) in
(
129
) proves (
13
).
∎

Lemma 14

(Cross bound for proxy-center drift)
.

Fix an outer iteration
k
, and let
\delta_{k}:=\lambda_{k+1}-\lambda_{k}\geq 0
. Let

v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}
be any vector chosen before updating
x_{k}
. Assume

\lambda_{k},\lambda_{k+1}\geq\lambda_{0}
, so that Proposition
2
applies.
For the fixed-
\lambda_{k}
proxy map
x\mapsto y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x)
, we use the following
local bounds.

(J0)

(Lipschitz continuity).

There exists a finite constant
\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}<\infty
such that, for all

x\in X
and all
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
,

\|\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x)u\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x)}\leq\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\|u\|_{2}.

(134)

(J1)

(Smoothness).

There exists a finite constant
\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}<\infty
such that

\Big\|y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\frac{\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}}{2}\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

(135)

Then, for any
j_{k}>0
,

\displaystyle\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Bigl(\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}+\xi\alpha_{k}j_{k}+\frac{(\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{2}\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr)\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigl(\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4j_{k}}(\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta})^{2}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{4}\alpha_{k}^{2}\Bigr)\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{y,k}^{2}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{2}}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{3}}.

(136)

Proof.

If
\delta_{k}=0
, the pure multiplier-drift term below vanishes. We therefore assume

\delta_{k}>0
; the case
\delta_{k}=0
follows by the same argument after omitting that term.

Decomposition:

Add and subtract
y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})
:

\displaystyle\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})\big\rangle}_{(I)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})\big\rangle}_{(II)}.

(137)

Multiplier drift:

By Cauchy–Schwarz in the anchored Dikin primal/dual pair,

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\big\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}.

(138)

Using the anchor-switch comparison between

y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})
and
y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})
,

\big\|u\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}\leq\kappa_{y,k}\big\|u\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})},

and applying Proposition
2
(i) with

(\lambda_{1},x_{1})=(\lambda_{k},x_{k+1})
and

(\lambda_{2},x_{2})=(\lambda_{k+1},x_{k+1})
, we get

\big\|y_{\lambda_{k+1},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})\big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})}\leq\frac{2\delta_{k}\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k+1}\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}\leq\frac{2\delta_{k}\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}.

Hence

(I)\leq\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\cdot\kappa_{y,k}\frac{2\delta_{k}\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}}.

Young’s inequality with

a:=\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*},\qquad b:=\kappa_{y,k}\frac{2\delta_{k}\ell_{f,0}^{\eta}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}\rho_{\psi}^{\eta}},\qquad c:=\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}},

gives

(I)\leq\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{y,k}^{2}(\ell_{f,0}^{\eta})^{2}}{(\rho_{\psi}^{\eta})^{2}}\frac{\delta_{k}}{\lambda_{k}^{3}}.

(139)

Fixed-
\lambda_{k}
motion:

Decompose
(II)
by Taylor expansion of the fixed-
\lambda_{k}
proxy map at
x_{k}
:

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big\langle v_{k},\,\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\big\rangle}_{(IIa)}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\Big\langle v_{k},\,y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\rangle}_{(IIb)}.

(140)

Since
x_{k+1}-x_{k}=-\xi\alpha_{k}q_{k}^{x}
, the linear term satisfies

(IIa)=-\xi\alpha_{k}\big\langle v_{k},\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})q_{k}^{x}\big\rangle.

By Cauchy–Schwarz and (
134
),

|(IIa)|\leq\xi\alpha_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}.

Young’s inequality with parameter
j_{k}>0
yields

(IIa)\leq\xi\alpha_{k}j_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}+\frac{\xi\alpha_{k}}{4j_{k}}(\ell_{\lambda,0}^{\eta})^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(141)

For the Taylor remainder, (
135
) and Cauchy–Schwarz give

\displaystyle(IIb)

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\Big\|y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k+1})-y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})-\nabla y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})(x_{k+1}-x_{k})\Big\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta}}{2}\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

(142)

As before,

\displaystyle\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}+2\lambda_{k}\ell_{g,0}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr).

(143)

Combining (
142
) and (
143
), and
absorbing the remaining product by Young’s inequality, yields

(IIb)\leq\frac{(\ell_{\lambda,1}^{\eta})^{2}}{2}\xi^{2}\bigl(\ell_{f,0}^{2}\alpha_{k}^{2}+4\ell_{g,0}^{2}\beta_{k}^{2}\bigr)\|v_{k}\|_{y_{\lambda_{k},\mu}^{\star}(x_{k}),*}^{2}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{4}\alpha_{k}^{2}\|q_{k}^{x}\|_{2}^{2}.

(144)

Combining (
139
), (
141
), and
(
144
) in (
137
) proves
(
14
).
∎
